Data instances with uncertain diagnoses were excluded from\nthis analysis. Following recommendations of the dataset authors who\nprovided predeﬁned participant-disjoint dataset splits, the tenth fold\nwas held out for model testing\n29.T h e ﬁrst 15 MI-positive ECGs in the\ntest set were annotated by a medical doctor to identify diagnostically\nrelevant sections for quantitative explainability evaluation.\nPromoters\nThe Human Non-TATA Promoters dataset (\"Promoters\") was extracted\nfrom the GenomicBenchmarks repository\n28. Data was originally pub-\nlished in ref. 27. 36,131 nucleotide sequences of 251 bases each were\nanalysed. Nucleotide sequences were converted to 4-channel one-hot\nvectors indicating adenine, cytosine, guanine and thymine. Inde-\nterminate bases were represented with zero vectors. Models were\nrequired to classify the promoter functionality of the sequence as\n“promoter\" ( N\ntrain = 12,355; Ntest =4 1 1 9 )o r “non-promoter\"\n(Ntrain =1 4 ,7 4 2 ;Ntest = 4915).\nCXR\nThe paediatric pneumonia chest X-ray dataset (\"CXR\") is a retro-\nspective cohort of patients aged between one andﬁve years, recorded\nin Guangzhou Women and Children ’s Medical Center, Guangzhou,\noriginally published in ref. 31. Chest X-ray images were recorded as\npart of routine care during diagnostic workup for suspected lower\nrespiratory tract infection. During data collection, clinicians screened\nthe images for quality and excluded those with severe artefacts or\ncorruption. Images were annotated by two expert physicians. Local\ninstitutional review board approvals were obtained. Images were loa-\nded in greyscale, rescaled to the [0, 1] intensity range and resized to\n128 × 128 pixels by bilinear interpolation. Models were required to\nclassify images as “normal\" (N\ntrain =1 3 4 9 ;Ntest = 234), “viral pneumo-\nnia\" ( Ntrain =2 5 3 8 ; Ntest =2 4 2 ) ,o r “bacterial pneumonia\"\n(Ntrain =1 3 4 5 ;Ntest =1 4 8 ) .\nOCT\nThe optical coherence tomography dataset (\"OCT\") is a retrospective\ncohort of adult patients from ﬁve ophthalmology institutions in the\nUSA and China recorded between 2013 and 2017 during routine care,\noriginally published in ref.31. Images were initially annotated by local\nmedical students, who had received OCT interpretation training.\nSubsequent annotation was performed by four ophthalmologists and\ntwo independent retinal specialists. Horizontal foveal cut images were\navailable in portable network graphics image format. Images were\nloaded in greyscale, rescaled to the [0, 1] intensity range, and resized to\n128 × 128 pixels by bilinear interpolation. Models were required to\nclassify images as either“normal\" (N\ntrain =2 9 2 6 ;Ntest =1 4 9 )o r“choroid\nneovascularisation\" (Ntrain =7 9 1 ;Ntest =1 7 8 ) .\nModel training\nThe FMNIST dataset was modelled using an MLP with 3 × 1000 hidden\nReLU-activated neurons. Sequential datasets (PTBXL-MI and Pro-\nmoters) were modelled by a 1D-CNN architecture of four convolutional\nblocks. Each convolutional block included two convolutional layers\nwith kernel dimensions 3 and strides of 1 and 2, respectively. Con-\nvolutional layers in the l-th block had 32 × 2\nl−1 ﬁlters. Convolutional\nimplementation is detailed in Supplementary section S.3. Convolu-\ntional outputs were aggregated by global average pooling in the\npenultimate layer. For gradient-descent-based learning algorithms,\nbatch normalisation layers were included between convolutional\nblocks. CIFAR2 modelling in Table 1 used a vision transformer\narchitecture\n42 operating on image patches of dimension 4 × 4, with a\nsequential stack of four multi-headed attention layers, each having 8\nheads, embedding dimension 64, and MLP dimension 64. 2D-CNN\narchitectures had kernel dimension 3 × 3 and the l-th convolutional\nblock had 16 × 2 l−1 ﬁlters (CXR and OCT), 32 × 2 l−1 ﬁlters (CIFAR2), or\n64 × 2l−1 ﬁlters (CIFAR10). The sign function was employed to generate\ntarget activations for Forward Projection models, such that\nezl =s i g nðal/C0 1Ql Þ +s i g nðyUl Þ: ð4Þ\nData augmentation was not performed in our experiments. Models\nwere ﬁtted using the PyTorch library. For SGD-based methods, early\nstopping was performed according to validation loss. Model weight\ninitialisation was random, and optimisation was performed with the\nAdam optimiser with a learning rate of 0.001, training to minimise\nvalidation loss by early stopping with a patience ofﬁve epochs. In few-\nshot experiments, the learning rate was reduced to 0.0001 for SGD-\nbased models, and a patience of ten epochs was employed. It is\nacknowledged that direct comparison of gradient-descent-based\ntraining with closed-form solutions is not strictly “like-for-like\".\nHowever, all efforts were made to ensure experimental conditions\nwere otherwise equivalent. Models wereﬁtted to minimise categorical\nor binary cross-entropy as appropriate. Implementations of Forward-\nForward, Local Supervision, Difference Target Propagation and\nPredictive Coding methods are detailed in supplementary section S.5.\nExplainability analysis\nModel architectures for explainability analysis were equivalent to\nthose used for the main experiments. expected one of {_SUPPORTED_ACTIVATIONS}.\"\n    )\n\n\ndef _solve_linear_system(A: torch.Tensor, B: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Solve A X = B using Gauss-Jordan elimination with partial pivoting.\n\n    Implemented with tensor ops rather than torch.linalg.solve to avoid a\n    LAPACK crash observed in this CPU-only environment.\n    \"\"\"\n    n = A.shape[0]\n    M = torch.cat([A, B], dim=1).clone()\n\n    for i in range(n):\n        col_tail = M[i:, i].abs()\n        pivot_offset = int(col_tail.argmax().item())\n        pivot_row = i + pivot_offset\n        if pivot_row != i:\n            swap = M[i].clone()\n            M[i] = M[pivot_row]\n            M[pivot_row] = swap\n\n        pivot = M[i, i].clone()\n        M[i] = M[i] / pivot\n\n        factors = M[:, i].clone()\n        factors[i] = 0\n        M = M - factors.unsqueeze(1) * M[i].unsqueeze(0)\n\n    return M[:, n:]\n\n\ndef fit_forward_projection(\n    X: torch.Tensor,\n    Y: torch.Tensor,\n    Qs: list[torch.Tensor],\n    Us: list[torch.Tensor],\n    ridge: float = 1e-3,\n    activation: str = \"tanh\",\n    target_nonlinearity: str = \"tanh\",\n) -> dict[str, list[torch.Tensor]]:\n    if activation not in _SUPPORTED_ACTIVATIONS:\n        raise ValueError(\n            f\"Unsupported activation {activation!r}; expected one of \"\n            f\"{_SUPPORTED_ACTIVATIONS}.\"\n        )\n    if target_nonlinearity not in _SUPPORTED_ACTIVATIONS:\n        raise ValueError(\n            f\"Unsupported target_nonlinearity {target_nonlinearity!r}; expected \"\n            f\"one of {_SUPPORTED_ACTIVATIONS}.\"\n        )\n    if len(Qs) != len(Us):\n        raise ValueError(\"Qs and Us must have the same length.\")\n\n    dtype = X.dtype\n    device = X.device\n\n    weights: list[torch.Tensor] = []\n    membranes: list[torch.Tensor] = []\n    activations: list[torch.Tensor] = [X]\n\n    A_prev = X\n    for l in range(len(Qs)):\n        Q_l = Qs[l].to(dtype=dtype, device=device)\n        U_l = Us[l].to(dtype=dtype, device=device)\n        Y_l = Y.to(dtype=dtype, device=device)\n\n        target = _apply_activation(A_prev @ Q_l, target_nonlinearity) + \\\n            _apply_activation(Y_l @ U_l, target_nonlinearity)\n\n        m_in = A_prev.shape[1]\n        gram = A_prev.transpose(0, 1) @ A_prev\n        identity = torch.eye(m_in, dtype=dtype, device=device)\n        gram = gram + ridge * identity\n        rhs = A_prev.transpose(0, 1) @ target\n\n        W_l = _solve_linear_system(gram, rhs)\n\n        Z_l = A_prev @ W_l\n        A_l = _apply_activation(Z_l, activation)\n\n        weights.append(W_l)\n        membranes.append(Z_l)\n        activations.append(A_l)\n\n        A_prev = A_l\n\n    return {\n        \"weights\": weights,\n        \"membranes\": membranes,\n        \"activations\": activations,\n    }\n\n\ndef forward_projection_predict(\n    X: torch.Tensor,\n    weights: list[torch.Tensor],\n    activation: str = \"tanh\",\n) -> tuple[list[torch.Tensor], list[torch.Tensor]]:\n    if activation not in _SUPPORTED_ACTIVATIONS:\n        raise ValueError(\n            f\"Unsupported activation {activation!r};
